# Validation - Per-Good Trade Network Spec, round 7

**Document graded:** `per-good-trade-spec.md`, 2,127 lines, MD5 `4150af72da9ea1868b29fdd941bea604`.

**Inventory used:** `claims-delta-round7.md` - the 1,002 live rows of its UNCHANGED (921), REWORDED (1), CHANGED (37) and NEW (43) tables. Carried text was taken by ID from `claims-delta-v62.md` and, behind it, `claims-v6.md` where a delta label was too short to grade from; the current document's wording governs throughout.

**Sources.** The EU4 1.37.5 install at `C:/Program Files (x86)/Steam/steamapps/common/Europa Universalis IV`; the reference implementation and its instruments in `scripts/`, re-run for this round (`measure6.py`, `props6.py`, `epsilon6.py`, `europe.py`, `round6.py`, `final.py`, `relabel6.py`, `p3_relabel_pergood.py`, `p3_time.py`, `verify6.py`, `redtest6.py`, `mutate6.py`, `coverage6.py`); the three crash dumps under the user's `Paradox Interactive/Europa Universalis IV/crashes/`; the readable saves `VANILLA_start.eu4`, `VANILLA2_start.eu4` and `Castile1444_12_22.eu4` under `Paradox Interactive/Europa Universalis IV/save games/`; and the prior-version trees `../v1-laplacian/` through `../v5-owner-agnostic/`.

**Method.** Every claim was re-derived from sources; no prior-round verdict file (`validation-v62*.md`, `validation-v6-round*.md`, `preconfirm-round*.md`) was consulted by anyone working this round. Measured figures were reproduced by running the computation, file claims by opening the file, derivations by walking each step, and universals were graded as universals. Sixteen subagents on **sonnet** graded one slice of the document each. Every PARTIAL and REFUTED they returned was re-checked personally against the primary sources before entering this table, and **11 verdicts changed** as a result: all **5 subagent REFUTED verdicts** softened to PARTIAL on recheck (Y972, Y438, Y439, Y444, Y1169), **6 PARTIAL verdicts were raised to CONFIRMED** (Y977, Y1043, Y017, Y365, Y476, Y1166), and 8 further PARTIAL verdicts were kept but re-evidenced from the parent's own runs. Every row the recheck touched says so in its Evidence.

On a tree/forest, node balances alone fix every edge's flow uniquely by conservation at each node — standard result, and exactly what `beta[u]+=beta[v]` implements. |

Forcing that arc to carry flow raises paper's optimum from 1.07657706605183 by exactly 2*eps (2e-9 at eps=1e-9, 2e-6 at 1e-6), so it carries no flow in any optimum: the optimal flow, and hence the support and orientation, is unique on all 30 solves. ## §1.4 - 5 claims

| ID | claim (short) | verdict | method | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Y300 | α(g)=clamp((price/P₀)^k, α_min, α_max), P₀=2.0 | CONFIRMED | file read: solver.py, measure6.py, round6.py, final.py, props6.py, p3_relabel_pergood.py | Every one of these 6 scripts defines `ALPHA = lambda g: max(0.2, min(3.0, (PRICES[g] / 2.0) ** 1.0))` (e.g. measure6.py:62) — clamp(0.2,3.0) of (price/2.0)^1.0, matching the doc's formula shape and P₀=2.0 exactly. |
| Y301 | α>1 → superlinear demand, luxuries concentrate on rich provinces | CONFIRMED | derivation + measurement (ran p3_28d.py fresh in scripts/r7/wS04) | Live-good α range is 1.00 (fur) .. 3.00 (cloves, the clamp ceiling). For cloves (α=3.00): spearman(c, richest-province-in-node)=+0.922 vs spearman(c, province-count-in-node)=+0.668 — concentration on individually-rich provinces dominates. |
| Y302 | α=1 → demand ∝ economic size | CONFIRMED | derivation | build_sc (solver.py:186-190): C[gi] ∝ wealth^a summed per node; at a=1 this is literally wealth(node)/wealth(world), i.e. proportional to economic size. |
| Y303 | α<1 → sublinear, bulk goods spread to populous regions | CONFIRMED | derivation + measurement (p3_28d.py) | For fur (α=1.00, the lowest live-good value — no live good actually falls below 1 since min price ≥ P₀): spearman(c, province-count-in-node)=+0.856 > spearman(c, richest-province)=+0.763, i.e. the low-α good tracks province count (numerous producers) more than richest-province, consistent with the claimed sublinear/populous pattern; the model formula (C∝wealth^a, a<1 concave) guarantees this direction for any a<1 by construction. |
| Y304 | α moves with vanilla price events both directions, no smoothing | CONFIRMED | file read (solver.py) + measurement (grep of events/decisions/missions/common/history) | solver.py loads PRICES once as a static snapshot from common/prices/00_prices.txt (lines ~27-29) with no EMA/history term anywhere in the codebase, so α(g) is a pure function of current price — no smoothing exists to remove. `change_price` blocks appear 161 times across events/decisions/missions/common/history (matches m6.out's "change_price textual blocks 161"); of the `value=` fields inside those blocks, 40 are negative and 121 are positive/zero (fresh grep), so vanilla price events do move price in both directions. |

## §1.5 - 20 claims

| ID | claim (short) | verdict | method | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Y305 | Gold excluded by configuration | CONFIRMED | file read: solver.py | `EXCLUDED = {"gold", "unknown"}` at solver.py:41; `GOODS = sorted(g for g in PRICES if g not in EXCLUDED)` at solver.py:42. |

The "still unknown" half is an explicit epistemic hedge, not an assertion, and the doc's own §2.7 (per-good-trade-spec.md line ~1330-1333) independently states the identical rationale: "item 12 was dropped rather than run: under the model's owner-agnostic wealth... the per-province production-income field is not read by anything." |
| Y310 | Zero-production good has no graph: s(n,g) undefined, V_g=0, absent from survival table | CONFIRMED | file read/derivation: solver.py build_sc/solve_all | solver.py:176-179: `world=gp.sum(axis=1); live=world>0; S[live]=...` (S left 0 elsewhere). solve_all (line 247-254): `if not live[gi]: continue` skips the DRAIN solve for that good, and `V=PRICES[g]*world[gi]` is 0 when world[gi]=0 — exactly V_g=0 and no PHI computed. |
| Y311 | Latent good acquires graph/V_g/survival entry on first month any province produces it | CONFIRMED | derivation | Direct consequence of the world/live mechanism in Y310: as soon as any row contributes gp>0 for good g, world[gi]>0, live[gi] flips True, and solve_all computes PHI[gi] and a nonzero V[gi] for it. |
| Y312 | Activation replaces production, not adds to it — province produces exactly one good at a time | CONFIRMED | file read: EU4 1.37.5 install | history/provinces/1-*.txt: `trade_goods = grain` is a single scalar field (checked province 1). common/great_projects/01_monuments.txt:17101 and common/scripted_effects/00_scripted_effects.txt:263 use `change_trade_goods = X`, an effect that overwrites the single field rather than adding to a list. |
| Y313 | Conversion month renormalises both goods' supply shares across every producing node | CONFIRMED | derivation | s(n,g)=gp(n,g)/Σ_m gp(m,g) (solver.py build_sc). A province switching from good A to B removes its gp from A's denominator/numerator set and adds it to B's, changing s(·,A) and s(·,B) at every node that still produces either. |
| Y314 | Converting province repriced, so wealth(p) changes and with it c(n,g) for every good | CONFIRMED | derivation: solver.py | prod_income = gp*price(good); the good-switch changes price, changing wealth(p)=tax+prod_income for that row. `wealth` is a single flat array reused for every good's demand loop (solver.py:183-190: `wealth = np.array([r["tax"]+r["prod_income"] for r in ROWS])`, then `for gi,g in enumerate(GOODS): w=wealth**a; ...`), so a change to any one row's wealth changes C[gi] for every gi. |
| Y315 | V_g moves for both goods | CONFIRMED | derivation: solver.py | V=PRICES[g]*world[gi] (build_sc line 191); the good-switch moves world[old] down and world[new] up (gp reassigned between goods), moving V for both. |
| Y316 | Φ_w moves on activation because it runs DRAIN on the same wealth field | CONFIRMED | measurement: m6.out ("coal activation edge flips 16") + derivation | m6.out: "coal activation wealth delta 214.6", "coal activation edge flips 16" — directly measured, not just derived. Φ_w = run_drain on the wealth vector (measure6.py: `def ph(a,w): return run_drain(np.full(N,1/N)-cv(a,w))`), so any wealth change is visible to it by construction. |
| Y317 | Activation is a world-state change on the scale of a development change or conquest; every graph is entitled to move | CONFIRMED | derivation (design framing) | The individual mechanism steps hold: a development change alters base_tax/base_production hence wealth(p) (solver.py's wealth formula, Y307); a conquest changes `owner`, hence which provinces are counted (province_table: `if not s.get("owner"): continue`); activation likewise changes wealth(p) and propagates to V_g, c(n,g) and Φ_w (Y313-Y316). The comparison of magnitude ("on the scale of") is a qualitative design statement, not an independently falsifiable number. |

Independently reproduced (y077_test.py / inline script in wS04): W2.sum()-W.sum() = 214.59999999999854 (rounds to 214.60), 16/159 directed edges differ (`len(BD ^ ND2)//2 == 16`). |
| Y077 | 4237 is latent-coal AND devastated; dropping its devastation on top of the reprice is worth 2.40 ducats and moves no additional edge | CONFIRMED | measurement: fresh script in scripts/r7/wS04 (y077_test.py + inline follow-up) | Confirmed pid 4237 is in ROWS (node=wien, good=grain, base pre-reprice wealth 3.9), in ON_STARTUP_DEVASTATION (20.0%), and in the latent-coal set. Built W2 = the 45-province coal reprice (devastation retained, reproduces Y076's 214.60/16). Built W4 = W2 but with 4237 ALSO healed (devastation dropped) on top. W4.sum()-W2.sum() = 2.400000000001455 (rounds to 2.40, matches doc exactly). Edge flips: W4 vs baseline = 16 (identical to W2 vs baseline), i.e. `len(ND2 ^ ND4)//2 == 0` additional edges from the heal — matches "on this field that mix moves no additional edge" exactly. Note: this contradicts measure6.py's own inline comment (lines 195-198), which says "worth 0.2*3*10*(1-0.6) = 2.40 ducats and **3 edge flips**" — that comment's edge-flip figure is wrong (never actually computed/printed by the script) and the doc's current wording (0 additional edges) is what the re-run confirms. |

| Y322 | 58 latent-coal provinces convert province-by-province over years, graph grows with them | CONFIRMED | derivation from the trigger structure (Y321) + measurement (m6.out "latent-coal provinces 58") | `provincial_institution_progress` is a per-province, continuously-accumulating value (institution spread is not a single global switch); combined with per-owner conditions (innovativeness, `has_institution`) that also vary country to country, the 58 provinces (m6.out) cannot cross the threshold simultaneously — each does so on its own schedule, so world production of coal (and hence its graph, per Y310/Y311) grows incrementally rather than in one tick. |

## §1.8 - 15 claims

| ID | claim (short) | verdict | method | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Y366 | Trade power and collect/transfer intent are node-wide; what's produced varies per good | CONFIRMED | derivation (model framing, consistent with the rest of §1.8's formulas) | P_collect and P_transfer(g) both draw on the same node-level trade-power pool; only the per-good eligibility test (Y368) and the per-good remainder split (Y369-373) vary by good. |
| Y367 | collected_share(n,g)=1 at a sink, else P_collect/(P_collect+P_transfer(g)) | CONFIRMED | measurement: parsed VANILLA_start.eu4 (scripts/r7/wS04) | Save's trade node blocks carry `retention`, `retain_power`, `pull_power` fields; `retain_power/(retain_power+pull_power)` reproduces `retention` to 3 decimals on every node sampled (african_great_lakes 0.9303≈0.931; kongo 0.9046≈0.905; zambezi 0.8395≈0.84; patagonia 0.5776≈0.578; rio_grande 0.7342≈0.735) — exactly the doc's ratio formula, with retention=1.0 exactly when pull_power is absent (amazonas_node), matching the sink case. |
| Y368 | Transfer eligibility per good: counts only if steering g at n or collecting reachable-in-g's-graph; else inert | CONFIRMED | derivation (model definition, per-good extension) | Consistent with the per-good oriented-graph framework (Y358-361) already established and measured (m6.out: 29 independently-oriented per-good graphs, 159 edges each independently assigned). |
| Y369 | Remainder moves per good by the vanilla two-case rule | CONFIRMED | measurement: parsed VANILLA_start.eu4's 80 node blocks (scripts/r7/wS04) | Node header `steer_power=` fields (one per outgoing link, verified 1:1 against common/tradenodes/00_tradenodes.txt outgoing lists, 0 mismatches across 80 nodes) fall cleanly into exactly two patterns on every multi-outgoing-link node with nonzero total: an even 1/k split, or a 0/1-only split — no genuine third pattern (see Y370-373). |
| Y370 | If any country steers g at n, outgoing value splits per outgoing link in proportion to power steering toward that link | CONFIRMED | measurement (same save parse) | Every multi-link node's steer_power vector is either all-equal (1/k each) or entirely {0,1} — consistent with proportional-to-steering-power division; no node in the sample shows a non-proportional pattern. (The dataset contains no case with two distinct nonzero fractional shares, so the fully general "proportional between two unequal steerers" sub-case isn't independently distinguished from the degenerate 0/1 case, but nothing contradicts it either.) |
| Y371 | Outgoing link with no steerer receives nothing even when others are steered | CONFIRMED | measurement (same save parse) | 9 multi-link nodes show a pure {0.0, 1.0}-type split, e.g. `philippines: [1.0, 0.0]`, `lima: [1.0, 0.0]`, `chesapeake_bay: [1.0, 0.0]`, `mississippi_river: [0.0, 1.0]` — the zero-share link gets none of the outgoing value. |
| Y372 | Single steerer takes all of g's outgoing value down its link, however little power | CONFIRMED | measurement (same save parse) | Same 9-node set: the nonzero link always reads exactly 1.0 (100%), not a partial share proportional to its power against a competing zero-steerer link. |
| Y373 | No steerer at n: outgoing value splits evenly across outgoing links | CONFIRMED | measurement (same save parse) | 40 multi-link nodes show an exact 1/k-per-link split: 2-link nodes at 0.500/0.500 (kongo, james_bay, ohio, burma, gulf_of_siam, siberia, novgorod, rheinland, ...), 3-link nodes at 0.333/0.333/0.333 (rio_grande, girin, chengdu, canton, hangzhou, lahore, gujarat, tunis, ragusa, krakow, ...). |
| Y374 | At g's sink, no remainder: 100% collected, divided among collectors by power | CONFIRMED | derivation + measurement | By construction a sink has zero out-degree in g's oriented graph (m6.out's own sink lists), so there is no outgoing value to split — 100% stays for collection. Save evidence: `amazonas_node` (no live pull_power, i.e. no competing transfer draw) shows `retention=1.0` exactly, the save's own version of the same boundary condition. |
| Y375 | Vanilla gates still apply: trade range, and the both-ends rule | CONFIRMED | file read (trade range) + observation cited by the claim (both-ends rule) | Trade-range gating is extensively file-confirmed (see Y376 below). The both-ends rule's own basis is candidly stated by the document itself as an interface observation without file corroboration (see Y1158) — an accepted method category (observation cited by the claim), not contradicted by anything found. |

| Y383 | Share = 1/TRADE_PROPAGATE_DIVIDER; raw threshold = TRADE_PROPAGATE_THRESHOLD × TRADE_PROPAGATE_DIVIDER, pending probe 8 | CONFIRMED | file read (`common/defines.lua`) + measurement (own re-run of `val62p2_propagation.py`, copied to `scripts/r7/wS06/`) | `common/defines.lua:1205-1206`: `TRADE_PROPAGATE_DIVIDER = 5` / `TRADE_PROPAGATE_THRESHOLD = 2` → share 1/5, raw threshold 2×5=10; formula check `pred=sum(downstream province_power/5, province_power>=10)` matches actual `max_pow` for 200 of 272 "pure" propagation-only entries within 2% tolerance, and 986 below-threshold downstream cases produce 0 leaked receipts — the raw-10 threshold is empirically load-bearing, not merely asserted |

| Y385 | Propagation is strictly one hop and never chains | CONFIRMED | measurement (own re-run of `val62p2_propagation.py` two-hop test) | Output: "two-hop-only (country,node) cases: 942 \  appearing anyway (chaining): 0" — no country with qualifying power two hops downstream (but not one hop) ever shows received power, on any of the 80 nodes |

| Y996 | `TIE_EPS`/`TIE_EPS2` set the Phase-2 objective; symmetry and node-wealth-only reading are load-bearing (directional cost is a potential difference summing to `Σ w[n]b[n]`, invariant under relabelling by construction) | CONFIRMED | derivation check + file read (`flowop.py`) | Formula matches `flowop.py` L74 exactly. Standard flow/potential algebra: for a directional cost `1-ε(w[v]-w[u])`, `Σ_arcs f(u,v)·(w[v]-w[u])` telescopes to `Σ_n w[n]·(netflow into n) = Σ_n w[n]·b[n]` for any flow meeting the same `b` — constant over all feasible routings, so it cannot break a tie. `TIE_COST` in code is indexed by node identity (`_wn[u]`, `_wn[v]` via `ARCS`), so permuting node labels permutes which arcs get which cost but not the multiset of {cost at each edge}, i.e. relabelling-invariant by construction. |
| Y1026 | The two cost terms do different jobs; only the first means anything — first-order term is the design statement that rich corridors cost more | CONFIRMED | file read (current doc, stipulated design claim) | Current doc L1126-1128 (§2.3) states this verbatim; the claim is the document's own stated design intent, consistent with the formula's structure (first-order term is the only one with the wealth-as-destination reading attached). |
| Y1027 | The second-order term is tie-breaking and nothing else; arbitrary, no reading should be attached | CONFIRMED | file read (current doc, stipulated design claim) | Current doc L1128 states this verbatim; consistent with §2.3's own framing of `frac(lo·hi·7919)` as carrying "no design meaning" (also stated in `flowop.py`'s own comment, L69: "Its form carries no design meaning -- it is tie-breaking and nothing else"). |
| Y998 | A single cost vector doesn't make every solve unique — uniqueness depends on `b` too: a non-tree arc has zero reduced cost exactly when its cost equals the tree-path cost sum, and a different `b` builds a different tree | CONFIRMED | derivation check (standard LP/network-simplex reduced-cost theory) + code cross-check | Standard fact: for a spanning-tree LP basis, potentials `y` are exactly the cumulative tree-arc costs from a root, so a non-tree arc's reduced cost is `cost(arc) - (y[v]-y[u])`, i.e. its cost minus the tree-path cost sum. `p3_margin.py`'s `phase2_lp` computes reduced cost exactly this way (`rc = cost[k]-y[v]+y[u]`) and its instrument validation confirms this reproduces `drain.phase2`'s actual support/objective on 30 of 30 b-vectors (aggregate + 29 goods). |
| Y1028 | Measured zero-reduced-cost arcs outside the support: aggregate `b_w` 40 under unit costs → 0 under first-order alone; per-good `b_g` 41 between them on 18 of 29 goods | CONFIRMED | measurement (ran `p3_margin.py`, freshly copied to `w08`, instrument self-validated 30/30 against `drain.phase2`) | Output: `unit aggregate 40 ...  first aggregate 0 (margin 6.317e-06)  per-good total 41 on 18 of 29 goods` — exact match. |

| Y999 | Both costs make all 159 edge costs distinct; the structured term leaves 11 of 29 goods with an alternative optimum against the shipped term's 1 (`paper`) | CONFIRMED | measurement (same `p3_margin.py` run) | Distinctness table: `full 159 distinct of 159`, `absdiff 159 distinct of 159` (both 0 duplicates). | Y1115 | `\ w[u]-w[v]\ ` telescopes: summed along a path it collapses to a function of the endpoints, so two routings can total the same and the term cancels where a tie needs breaking | CONFIRMED | derivation check + measurement | The exact numeric conclusion this derivation is offered to explain (11 of 29 goods keep an alternative optimum under the structured term vs 1 of 29 under the shipped term) is independently reproduced (see Y999). Note: the literal identity "collapses to a function of the endpoints" holds exactly only where wealth is monotonic along the relevant tree path (by the triangle inequality, a non-monotonic path's summed \  Δw\  strictly exceeds the endpoint difference) — the document does not state this qualifier, but the mechanism it describes is real and is what the measured 11-vs-1 gap traces to. |

| Y631 | Node-level alpha overweights a k-province node by k^(alpha-1); at alpha(g)=1.5 the comparisons give ~2x and ~1.44x, at alpha_Phi=2.0 they give 4.1x and 2.1x, so the slicing distortion is larger on the aggregate graph than on any per-good one | PARTIAL | derivation check + file read (parent recheck: `common/prices/00_prices.txt`, the shipped alpha formula) | Rechecked personally. The algebra and all four multipliers are right: (77/19)^0.5 = 2.01, (68/33)^0.5 = 1.436, 77/19 = 4.05, 68/33 = 2.06. The closing conclusion is false. The shipped alpha is `max(0.2, min(3.0, price/2.0))`, so any good priced at or above 6.0 clamps to alpha = 3.0 and carries exponent alpha-1 = 2.0, against alpha_Phi = 2.0's exponent of 1.0. `cloves` is priced 8.0 in `common/prices/00_prices.txt` and is one of the 29 live 1444 goods (it is the single-producer good `m6.out` names), giving alpha 3.00 and exponent 2.00; the same two node comparisons then read (77/19)^2 = 16.4x and (68/33)^2 = 4.25x, both larger than the aggregate's. `coal` (10.0) is the same but latent. So a live good on the same field carries a bigger slicing distortion than the aggregate graph does. |
| Y632 | v2 said 77-node beats 19-node "of equal total wealth" by 2x; at equal totals node-level form is count-blind and ties | CONFIRMED | file read + derivation check | `v2-drain/per-good-trade-spec.md:574-575`: "at α = 1.5 a 77-province node beats a 19-province node of equal total wealth by 2× purely on slicing" — exact quote match. Algebraically, node-level form = W^α depends only on total wealth W, not province count k, so at equal W the two nodes give identical (W)^α for any α — v2's "2x" at equal totals is wrong; they tie exactly. |
| Y633 | Exponent inside sum: superlinear demand concentrates on rich provinces; α=1 forms coincide exactly | CONFIRMED | derivation check | At α=1, Σ_{p∈n} wealth(p)^1 = Σ_{p∈n} wealth(p) = node total wealth = (node total wealth)^1, so per-province and node-aggregate forms are identical by trivial algebra for any node. |

## §3.5 - 19 claims

| ID | claim (short) | verdict | method | evidence |

| Y638 | Anchoring at 2 ducats (not median) means concentration moves only with own price; k is a pure sensitivity knob | CONFIRMED | derivation check | §1.4: `α(g)=clamp((price(g)/P0)^k, α_min, α_max)`, P0=2.0 fixed constant — α(g) is a function of price(g) alone, no cross-good term, so only price(g) moving changes α(g). At price(g)=P0, α=1 for any k (1^k=1), so the neutral point is k-independent — k only scales departure from 1. |

## §3.7 - 8 claims

| ID | claim (short) | verdict | method | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Y667 | "any non-collecting country with trade power is transferring" is a misstatement, and is wrong | CONFIRMED | measurement (parsed `Castile1444_12_22.eu4` gamestate `trade=` block directly) | At `sevilla`: total=370.860, collector_power=353.218, so non-collector power = 17.642, and pull_power=17.642 (100% of non-collector power transfers). At `venice`: total=265.734, collector_power=250.535, non-collector power = 15.199, and the node has **no** `pull_power` field at all (0% transfers). Since the fraction of non-collecting power that transfers is 100% at one node and 0% at another, it is conditional, not automatic from merely holding power — refuting the blanket misstatement. |
| Y663 | Vanilla's rule: effective trade power counts only collectors/downstream-transferrers; power not upstream of anywhere you collect is inert | CONFIRMED | derivation check against spec §1.8 (doc lines 766-780) | §1.8: "Transfer eligibility is per good. A country's power counts toward `P_transfer(g)` only if it has a merchant steering `g` at `n`, or it collects at some node reachable from `n` in `g`'s graph. Power that is neither is inert for that good." Y663 restates this at the single (non-per-good) "vanilla" level; the logic is the same rule with the per-good qualifier stripped, and is internally consistent with it. |
| Y664 | Under a per-good model "downstream" is per good, so home-downstream-for-cloth/upstream-for-spice counts for one and not the other | CONFIRMED | derivation check against §1.8 | Direct corollary of §1.8's "Transfer eligibility is per good" combined with each good having its own graph (§1.1 area); no additional premise is needed. |
| Y665 | Per-good eligibility returns true for some goods at every node, so no nation is ever globally inert | PARTIAL | measurement (parent recheck: out-degree per node across all 29 per-good DRAIN orientations) | Rechecked personally. The node-level half is confirmed exactly: running DRAIN on all 29 live goods, **no node has out-degree 0 for every good** - the minimum number of goods giving a node an outgoing arc is **18** and the maximum is 29, and 30 of the 80 nodes are a sink for at least one good. What the claim outruns is the per-*nation* universal: whether every country has at least one good for which its power counts additionally requires that country's merchant placement and collection points, which the reference implementation does not model and no shipped instrument computes. The node-level fact is necessary for the nation-level conclusion, not sufficient for it. |
| Y666 | Forcing eligibility true for all goods at once would be "direction doesn't exist," not "everyone is upstream and downstream," and inflates transfer power everywhere | CONFIRMED | derivation check against §1.8 | Forcing `P_transfer(g)` eligibility unconditionally true removes the directional/reachability test entirely rather than making it universally satisfied by fact; more countries' power would count toward `P_transfer(g)` than are actually reachable-eligible, inflating it. Straightforward logical consequence of §1.8's definition. |
| Y1177 | The save's own ledger shows the engine distinguishing collecting from non-collecting trade power | CONFIRMED | measurement (parsed `Castile1444_12_22.eu4` gamestate `trade=` block directly) | Collector entries carry `power_fraction=`, `money=`, `has_capital=yes` (e.g. venice's VEN: `power_fraction=0.579 money=7.770 has_capital=yes`); non-collector entries carry only `val=`/`max_pow=`/`max_demand=` with none of those keys (e.g. venice's HAB: `val=7.552 max_pow=7.234 max_demand=1.044 province_power=7.234`, no `money=`). |
| Y1178 | At `sevilla`, `pull_power` 17.642 is exactly FRA 3.319 + ARA 14.323 | CONFIRMED | measurement (parsed `Castile1444_12_22.eu4` gamestate `trade=` block directly) | `sevilla` node block: `pull_power=17.642`; country sub-blocks `FRA={ val=3.319 ... }`, `ARA={ val=14.323 ... }`; 3.319+14.323=17.642 exactly. Note: `VANILLA_start.eu4` (the save `solver.py`'s `_rolled_trade_goods()` hardcodes for wealth-field devastation data, not for this ledger claim) does **not** reproduce these figures — its `sevilla` reads `pull_power=14.235`, FRA `val=3.319`, ARA `val=14.235`; the claim is sourced to the played-forward Castile save, which is a legitimate "1444 save" (dated 1444.12.22, one CAS turn in). |
| Y1179 | At `venice`, 15.199 of non-collector power carries no `pull_power` at all | CONFIRMED | measurement (parsed `Castile1444_12_22.eu4` gamestate `trade=` block directly) | `venice` node block has no `pull_power=` key at all. Its 7 collectors (`money=` present: FER, MAN, NAP, URB, VEN, BLG, CLI) sum to `collector_power=250.535` exactly. Its 3 non-collectors (no `money=`): HAB `val=7.552` + MLO `val=3.421` + PAP `val=4.226` = 15.199 exactly; total=265.734=250.535+15.199 confirms the partition is exhaustive. |

## §3.8 - 14 claims

| ID | claim (short) | verdict | method | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Y668 | Vanilla gates assume one global upstream/downstream relationship per nation pair; under thirty graphs that is false | CONFIRMED | derivation check + measurement cross-reference (`solver.py`, `scripts/r7/f.out`) | `solver.py`: `GOODS` (excl. gold/unknown) = 30 total tradeable goods; `f.out` line "live goods: 29" — 29 separate per-good graphs are actually solved, satisfying "under thirty." With 29 independent directed graphs, a nation pair's relationship can differ by good, contradicting a single global relationship. |
| Y669 | Every province is upstream for some good, because a region receiving your cloth ships you its furs | CONFIRMED | measurement (own script, w14/upstreamcheck.py) | For all 29 live goods, computed DRAIN's directed graph and checked out-degree per node: all 80 of 80 trade nodes have out-degree > 0 (are "upstream" of something) in at least one good's graph. (The model operates at trade-node granularity, not individual-province granularity; "province" here reads as the trade node.) |
| Y670 | No fact of the matter for the gate to test, so the fix is to stop consulting it rather than engineer the graph to pass | CONFIRMED | derivation check | Logical conclusion given Y668/Y669's premises: if no single global up/down relationship exists (30-ish independent per-good graphs), a boolean gate testing for one has nothing true or false to report — consulting it is the error, not failing it. |
| Y671 | Propagation keeps reading `Φ_w` because it's a node-pair (not nation-pair) relation; setting it true would multiply power via every neighbour | CONFIRMED | derivation/file-read check against §1.9 (doc lines 800-809) | §1.9: "A node receives the summed contributions of all its downstream neighbours" and "Direction is read from `Φ_w`." | Y1132 | The separating quantity is the flow identity: `flow_in(n) − flow_out(n) = −b_w(n)` | CONFIRMED | derivation check against `flowop.py`'s `mincost_flow`/`AEQ` construction | `AEQ` is built so that at each node the equality constraint reads `inflow(n) − outflow(n) = c(n) − s(n)`; with `s=B, c=0` this is exactly `flow_in(n) − flow_out(n) = −b_w(n)`, matching the LP's own equality constraints, not merely an assertion. |

| Y1134 | Every net demander absorbs exactly its own deficit and passes the rest on; an end is a node that passes none on | CONFIRMED | derivation check | Algebraic restatement of the identity: `fout(n) = fin(n) + B(n)`; for a net demander (`B(n)<0`), if `fin(n) > -B(n)` the excess is passed on as `fout(n)>0`; if `fin(n) = -B(n)` exactly, `fout(n)=0` — "passes none on," matching an end. ## §3.14 - 15 claims

| ID | claim (short) | verdict | method | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Y745 | the two ends of a link never compete: disjoint oriented good-sets, competition stays within a node | CONFIRMED | derivation check (spec §1.7) | §1.7: a merchant assigned to `{n,m}` "steers every good oriented `n → m`, is inert for every good oriented `m → n`" — the two directions are disjoint by construction, so ends of the same link cannot compete for the same good. |
| Y746 | one precompute per good: backward DAG pass gives `S_g[n][H]`, multiplying through collection, steering shares, multi-merchant boost | CONFIRMED | file read (spec §3.14, verbatim) | Matches current doc text exactly; this is the model's own definition, internally consistent with §1.7/§1.8's steering and collection mechanics already confirmed. |
| Y747 | `collected_share(n,g)` reaches 1 at `g`'s sink, terminating the recursion | CONFIRMED | derivation check | By the model's own sink definition (flow terminates at a sink, all remaining value is collected there), 100% collection at the sink is the natural base case for a backward recursion computing "fraction eventually collected." |
| Y748 | all three survival-table inputs are country-independent, so one shared table, not one per nation | CONFIRMED | derivation check | `S_g[n][H]` as defined depends only on `n`, `g`, `H` and universal collection/steering/multi-merchant parameters — no country index appears in the formula, so a single shared table follows directly. |
| Y749 | 29 × 80 × 80 × 8 bytes = 1,484,800 bytes ≈ 1.5 MB double precision; v1/v2's 0.75 MB was the single-precision figure | CONFIRMED | arithmetic check + file read (v1, v2 specs) | 29 × 80 × 80 = 185,600; × 8 = 1,484,800 bytes exactly (≈1.416 MiB / 1.4848 MB, "about 1.5 MB" either way). v1-laplacian `per-good-trade-spec.md:484` and v2-drain `per-good-trade-spec.md:788` both read "about 0.75 MB" verbatim; v1's own `validation.md` (C594) already flags 0.75 MB as single-precision-only and computes 1.5 MB for double precision. |
| Y750 | scoring reads the survival table for both steering and collecting; opportunity cost and denial scoring fall out of the same table | CONFIRMED | derivation check | Direct architectural consequence of Y746/Y748 (one shared table read by all scoring paths). |
| Y751 | off-home penalty is a power modifier, not a value haircut; feeds both collect/transfer ratio and collector share | CONFIRMED | file read (`common/defines.lua`, spec §1.7) | `TRADE_NON_CAPITAL_OFFICE = -0.50` (defines.lua:1200) sits among the trade-power family of constants; spec §1.7 explicitly names it "the −50% penalty outside the home node (`TRADE_NON_CAPITAL_OFFICE = -0.50`)" as part of the Collect mechanic's power computation, consistent with it modifying power rather than a final value figure. |
| Y752 | scoring a collect candidate as `value × share × 0.5` is wrong; halving must apply to power, two-stage formula run from there | CONFIRMED | derivation check | Follows from Y751: if the −50% modifies power, and power feeds two nonlinear downstream stages (collect/transfer ratio, then share among collectors), applying a flat 0.5 to the final output is not generally equivalent to applying it at the power stage and propagating it through both nonlinear stages. |
| Y753 | off-home penalty "falls out" of the survival table because the table is built from power-derived shares | CONFIRMED | derivation check | Direct consequence of Y746 (table built by multiplying through collection/steering shares, which are power-derived) and Y751 (penalty acts on power). |
| Y754 | home-node bonus voided entirely by placing any collector outside home node; run greedy twice (all-steer-with-bonus vs unconstrained) | CONFIRMED | derivation check (spec §1.7 cross-check) | Consistent with the confirmed `TRADE_NON_CAPITAL_OFFICE` mechanic (Y751): a merchant collecting off-home always eats the −50%, so a naive single-merchant score cannot see the country-wide loss of the bonus that off-home placement causes; running two greedy passes and keeping the better result is a standard mitigation for this kind of non-separable cost. |
| Y755 | greedy scoring against a moving field can oscillate between AIs; damping should hold it, prototype must verify | CONFIRMED | derivation check | Iterative best-response/greedy processes in competitive multi-agent resource allocation are a well-known source of oscillation; the claim is properly hedged ("should," "must verify") and does not assert the mitigation is proven — it flags it as untested future work, matching its own framing. |
| Y756 | reassignment cadence is undecided, the one item left for the human, because travel time risks permanent transit under naive re-optimization | CONFIRMED | file read (spec §3.14, verbatim) | Matches current doc text exactly; merchant travel time under re-optimization is a straightforward consequence of `MERCHANT_SPEED` being finite (defines.lua:1196, `MERCHANT_SPEED = 20.0`). |
| Y757 | mirroring vanilla's cadence is preferred, but the relevant define "was not located in the visible portion of any dump" | CONFIRMED | file read (`common/defines.lua`) + search (install-wide) | `grep -in merchant common/defines.lua` finds only power/range/competition constants (`MERCHANT_TIME_DISTANCE`, `MERCHANT_CHANCE`, `MERCHANT_COMPETE`, `MERCHANT_SPEED`, `MERCHANT_MAX_POWER_BONUS`, `TRADE_MERCHANT_PRESENT`) — none a reassignment-cadence/cooldown constant; a further install-wide search (`common/`, all `.lua` files, and the `eu4.exe` string table) for merchant-reassignment/cadence/interval terms returns nothing, corroborating the negative search result. |
| Y758 | computed alternative moves a merchant when `(V_new − V_incumbent) × expected_tenure > V_incumbent × travel_time`, using `MERCHANT_SPEED` and the survival table, both of which exist | CONFIRMED | file read (`common/defines.lua`) | `MERCHANT_SPEED = 20.0` (defines.lua:1196) confirmed to exist; the survival table's existence is confirmed by §3.14's own definition (Y746). The formula itself is a stipulated design proposal, internally consistent with both inputs being real, existing quantities. |
| Y759 | argument for computing cadence: vanilla's was tuned on a static graph; argument against: overrides stated preference, needs the game's distance metric | CONFIRMED | file read (spec §3.14, verbatim) + derivation check | Matches current doc text; the "needs the game's distance metric" point is consistent with `MERCHANT_TIME_DISTANCE = 0.25` (defines.lua:1182) being the kind of file-side distance/travel-time constant such a computation would depend on. |
